THE FOUR HOSTS declared TWO signatures - `togglePause(opts)` three times and `togglePause(setPlayerPos, opts)` once - and `routeAction`'s Escape arm spelt it the odd one's way, so the other three got the key router's `null` default where their options should be; `opts = {}` is a default parameter and a default parameter does not fire on `null`. One signature now, `setPlayerPos` inside the bag, `pauseOpts` the one reader. And the reason it reached a player: **AUDIT-MACK's own fall-through had been written ABOVE `world.js`'s Escape arm**, so the table claimed Escape first and the ladder's arm - which passes no options and could never have crashed - was unreachable. A tail that is not last is not a tail. **L1b**, found inside it: `togglePause`'s parameter was called `opts` and SHADOWED `buildDungeonContext`'s host bag, so the dungeon pause screen's Quests tab and its relock had always read the method's own argument - dead, and looking wired. **L2** (a banker crash) is NOT reproduced and is recorded as a hypothesis, not a finding: the window contract is green and the likeliest reading is that it IS L1, met by pressing Escape out of the banker's panel. **L3**: the browser menu was shut on the CANVAS by four hosts while THIRTEEN DOM surfaces sat over it and two shut it themselves - one document-level guard now, text entry excepted - and the port had NO `beforeunload` anywhere, so every gesture a browser reads as Back took the session in silence (`systems/unloadGuard.js`, many arms and one answer, stood down by `exitToTitleMenu` before it navigates). **L4**: `restorePlayer` ended a lookup in `.filter(Boolean)` and a stock spell is a bare SPELLS.STD index, so a load in the seconds before `loadMagicRegistries` resolved swept every one of them and the next save wrote the loss down - the host waits for its table now, and an entry a restore cannot read is HELD and written back out rather than dropped. 20 mutants, 20 killed. **The standing lesson is a queue rather than a measurement:** three of the four findings, and two of AUDIT-CHATR's from the day before, are each exactly one eslint rule the tree already applies to `server/src` and does not apply to `src` - `no-shadow` (111 sites) and `no-unused-vars` (172). **AUDIT-MACL** the same day (Mac: "Do an audit on everything before we merge"): SEVEN findings against MAC-L itself, every one in the WIRING rather than the law. `resolvePendingSpells` had no caller and this page's own record claimed it did; MAC-L4's `await` landed between `if (_loading) return;` and `_loading = true`, so a fix for a silent data loss opened a re-entry two F9s could both walk through; two hosts armed the unload guard with a blanket `() => true` (false for the whole of chargen) and three chargen-Cancel reloads navigated without standing it down; L1 turned out to have a SECOND route nobody named (the large HUD's options panel routes Escape by MOUSE); the "two surfaces that shut the menu themselves" was ONE, and under that miscount sat a real hazard - two surfaces use the right button as a genuine control, so the guard must preventDefault and must never stopPropagation; L2 was re-opened and DRIVEN through a real window stack in both arms rather than closed on a reading, still not reproduced but on better evidence; and the `beforeunload` guard's bfcache cost on Firefox is now written into the file as a trade taken deliberately rather than an accident. 8 mutants, 8 killed. **The habit worth naming: four consecutive slices where the audit's findings sat in the wiring rather than the logic - this port's code is more reliable than this port's connections between code.**

- `01-Overview/Mac-Bugs-M.md` - CLOSED 2026-09-17: THE TWO NUMBERS AN ITEM IS FOR (Mac: "Damage values arent showing on weapon tool tips. Also, not sure if armor has values either"). Both true, and the shape under them is the FOURTH of its kind this week: **the producer existed and had one reader.** `weaponDamageString` and `armourModString` have been in `systems/itemInfo.js` since U25, faithful to DFU (an iron dagger's `0 - 5` is not an off-by-one - iron's -1 modifier is `CalculateWeaponMaterialModifier`, checked against the port's own table before it was called a bug), and between them they had exactly ONE reader: `expandItemInfo`'s `%wdm`/`%mod`, which fill the CLASSIC popup's TEXT.RSC record. The enhanced card draws no record at all - it builds its rows from `itemLine` - so the popup told a player a dwarven longsword hits for 4-18 and the card told them what it weighed. Three hovers were affected and the most literal reading of "tool tips" was the worst: the GRID TILE's own `title`, which said the name and nothing else. ONE PRODUCER, TWO PRESENTERS: the numbers are shared so the two surfaces cannot print different ones for the same sword, but the QUESTION is not - the macro asks "what does %wdm expand to" and lets the record decline (an arrow's 1011 has no damage line), while a card with no record must ask "should there be a Damage row", which is why the arrow test lives in the presenter. And the three hovers share ONE suffix builder rather than each spelling the `??`. **The claim that was not checked:** Mac's "not sure" was answered with "the classic popup was always right" - which, at the moment it was said, was a READING. `%wdm` and `%mod` had no test anywhere in the tree. They are pinned now, with the cross-check that both surfaces agree. An answer to "not sure" that is itself unverified is not an answer. 11 mutants, 11 killed.

- `01-Overview/Incident-2026-09-01.md` - CLOSED 2026-09-01, and its four **Standing lessons are binding process law** for every slice after it: (1) the palette is SHARED and mutable and any palettized-IMG load can destroy it - `incident_texture.test.js`'s site sweep is the guard, and a new palettized-IMG consumer either mints its own palette or extends that sweep; (2) a FIRST-EVER-RENDERED path is a release risk however lawful the code (winter-at-boot was correct 1:1 behaviour and still broke the live site), so any change that lights a never-drawn path gets the owner's eyes before merge - the suite cannot see pixels; (3) keep merging through GATED PRs, because deploy-and-eyeball bisects in O(log n) only when the known-good checkpoints exist; (4) never plain-merge a revert commit into the branch that carries the fix - `-s ours` or a tree-restore, since the three-way silently prefers the revert wherever the fix branch is quiet. The record itself: two texture breakages in one afternoon, both invisible to 5,600+ green tests.

- `01-Overview/Hardening.md` - THE HARDENING PROGRAM (HARD, 2026-09-14, Mac: "I really want to focus on making everything clean, hardened and not spaghetti ... I think i want to take a break of additions and do this right"). The plan and the running record for a pass that adds no features. Its principle, learned from AUDIT 66 and from `audit24_lifetimes.test.js` before it: A RULE ENFORCED BY AN ENUMERATION IS A RULE ENFORCED BY MEMORY - a gate that pins the defects an audit found protects the past, and the next instance of the same class ships green. Every slice converts a remembered rule into a DERIVED one that reads the source, finds every site the rule applies to, and fails closed on the undecided ones. HARD1 (the generative lifetime gate), HARD2 (one contract per cross-cutting system, so a law is not wired four times by hand - its first seam is the activation race), HARD3 (types at the three seams where a wrong shape crashes) and HARD4 (the vendored-work registry) are SHIPPED; HARD5 (Home.md back to an index) is written up there with its risks, and so are HARD2's remaining seams. **AUDIT-HARD (2026-09-15, Mac: "Lets audit this before merging")** read the four slices against themselves and found nine things, of which one was a defect in shipped code and three were holes in the gates. The headline: HARD1's very first fix was a DOUBLE FREE - the pool it named is a HAND-OFF, not a leak - and it stayed green because the ownership check was reading COMMENTS. Three more gates claimed to derive while quietly enumerating directories. All nine paid; the lesson recorded there is that A GATE IS CODE AND ROTS THE WAY CODE ROTS, so the gates need auditing on the same cycle as the port.
- `01-Overview/Mod-Registry.md` - THE VENDORED-WORK REGISTRY (HARD4, 2026-09-15). One row per directory under `vendor/` - what the port takes, the upstream author and version, the bundle or commit it was read from, the licence or permission, the port slice, the parity date and the bible page - plus the known deviations per row. `test/hard4_registry.test.js` derives the row list from the filesystem, so a new vendored work cannot arrive without one, and cross-checks each version and author against the vendored `.dfmod.json` so a refreshed bundle cannot drift from its row. WHAT READING THE FOURTEEN TOGETHER FOUND: SIX of them carry an unfilled `[Mac: paste the text of the permission...]` line, each sitting under a sentence that says permission WAS granted. That is a record problem and not a licence one - Mac's word with a date settles whether the port may carry the files - but the evidence was never pasted in, and one README at a time nobody would ever see that it had happened six times. Those rows read RECORD OPEN, and the gate holds the mark and the placeholder in step in BOTH directions. Also surfaced: PCAAO's port is built from the shipped 1.44 bundle decompiled, while the last single-file source in its repository is 1.40.

The state table is one law rather than 168 rows (0 of 168 deviate, checked mechanically and pinned against the table vendored out of the assembly); 3035 sprites re-encoded as indexed PNG, 19.72 MB to 8.39 MB, lossless for every drawn pixel. Six findings. F1: the sprite's own clock had no caller, so the body never animated. F2: `loadSettings` had no caller, so every dial on the pane was inert and the camera ran on the vendored defaults. F3: no host passed `dt`, so MoveTowards stepped nothing and the camera parked at the player's FEET. F4: no host passed `weaponReady`, so all three CameraOverride arms were unreachable code. F5: the up vector was `right x forward`, inverted. F6: four exports called by nothing. Every one of them had passed a green suite, because every pin drove the UNIT and none drove the WIRING - the F-SING lesson for the fifth time. F1-F5 are fixed and each is pinned by driving `mwViewFrame`, the only path a player has; F6 is recorded and GATED by a derived dead-export ledger. **The 1:1 claim is corrected in seven places**: the assembly carries 62 authored methods and the port implements thirteen, and `test/eotb_scope.test.js` now holds all 62 rows with a verdict each, so the claim is checkable rather than asserted. 59 pins; 90 mutants run, 89 killed - the arc's own 59 (58 killed) plus AUDIT-EOTB's 31, whose single survivor was `ready()`'s `firstUp` clause and is now killed by F7. The one that stands is redundancy in the MOD (a scroll reset the other path already does), kept because the IL keeps it and recorded so nobody simplifies it on the grounds that no test fails. The art has NOT been seen on a GPU. **AUDIT-EOTB2 (2026-09-16, Mac: "Do an audit on eye of the beholder. Ensure its integrated 1:1. No half assed work")**: THE BODY WAS A STATUE. The rig registered two fields and the hosts handed one, so nothing the table chooser reads was ever written - the sprite stood in Idle for ever, unarmed, undying, a man's size in the saddle, with the first-person weapon drawn over it (its third-person gate asked the Morrowind arm alone), StartInThirdPerson dead with no caller, the SwitchShoulder key declared and never polled, and the sprite cache keyed by record alone so the horse archives collided with the on-foot ones. Six wiring findings fixed, ONE HOME for the record (the rig's, off the motion bag its camera thunk already carries). Then the honest half: THE ASSEMBLY IS NOT IN THE TREE and cannot be fetched from the container, so the sixteen members the arc left NOT DONE (the attack and death one-shots and their three coroutines, the footstep sync, the auto-toggle table and its two transition rows, the two hides) are ported from the SETTINGS' own names and option labels, marked `[SETTINGS]` at every site and pinned so a later reading of the IL corrects a test rather than a player. 29 of 62 now; the cart, the per-sprite offsets, the one-shots' tick and TorchOffset wait on the `.zip` Mac holds. The one boot-visible change: a player without Morrowind data starts behind their own shoulder, as the mod ships. 17 pins. Still NOT SEEN ON A GPU. **EOTB-FLIP (2026-09-16, Mac: "The character is upside down (classic sprite)")** - the first thing Mac's eye found: the body decoded its art through a canvas of its own and uploaded the raster top row first to a billboard that samples bottom-up, the class `formats/color32Order.js` records for the fourth time. - `07-Rendering/Enhanced-Lighting-Arc.md` - ACTIVE, opened 2026-09-17 (Mac, on Better Ambience's dungeon fog: "we might need to update our enhanced lighting ... or we can take it a step further an enhance our lighting system tenfold" - tiers "1, 2, and 3"). EL1 FOUNDATIONS SHIPPED the same day: `render/enhancedLighting.js`, the lane the renderer installs over its classic world program set (`setLightingLane`, compiled once per key and kept across swaps; the classic shaders untouched to the byte and never compiled beside the lane's on a classic page) - sRGB decode of every texel and every scene colour the renderer uploads, so lights add in linear; lanterns on a windowed inverse square (`2 / (1 + 16 x^2)` times `(1 - x^4)^2`, zero at the range) and forty-eight of them (the cap is the installed set's, read by every host's `nearestLights`); the city's and the dungeon's white lanterns given a ~1900 K flame at the host's intensity; exposure 1.4 then an extended Reinhard (white 4) that keeps a 0.12 ambient at 0.145 and blooms a torch's near field; the analytic single scattering of each lantern along the view ray, windowed to its range and scaled by the fog's own density, so the foggy dungeon shows every torch as a halo and clear air shows nothing; the fog colour blended decoded and re-encoded so a fogged fragment is the sky's colour; the far ring on the same lane and exposure. Switch `enhanced-lighting` (on by default, `?lighting=classic` the kill door, `?exposure=` the tuning door). 11 pins, the uniform sweep widened to the lane's file and the far ring's; 41 mutants, 40 killed, 1 equivalent as recorded. EL2 SHADOWS SHIPPED the same day: `render/shadowPass.js` - the renderer has no scene graph, so the pass records what the world pass draws (a pooled list of meshes with copied matrices, terrain surfaces, billboard batch lists) and replays it depth-only from the light at the top of the next frame's beginFrame, before the clear; outdoors a two-cascade orthographic sun map (40 and 240 units at 2048^2, a depth texture array with hardware compare, texel-snapped), indoors a six-face 512^2 cube map from the nearest lantern that is not the eye's own, the shader's depth reference pinned equal to the face's projected depth; the lane's shaders read both (the sun map on the sun term with 3x3 PCF, the cube on its one lantern; a flat reads its shadow at its base once), the depth programs are the renderer's own vertex shaders over a nothing FS and a cutout FS, culling off under the light. Recorded limits: a caster the frame did not draw (the hosts' frustum cull), the character rigs, the water, the moon. 8 pins; 40 mutants, 40 killed. EL3 DEPTH AND AIR SHIPPED the same day: `render/airPass.js` - a depth image of the world from the camera (the shadow pass's records replayed under the camera's view-projection, at the top of beginFrame after the maps), and off it SSAO at half resolution (twelve hemisphere samples off the reconstructed position and a normal flipped to face the eye, hashed per pixel, range-checked, box-blurred; the lane's mesh, terrain and character shaders multiply their AMBIENT alone by it), bloom at a quarter sourced from the emitters (the records replayed with an emission-only program) and a glare sprite per lantern hidden by the depth image, blurred twice, and the sun's shafts (the sky mask toward the sun's screen position, 32 taps with decay), the two composited additively by the frame's first screen-space draw over the world viewport. THE DEPARTURE RECORDED: the plan's RGBA16F world target and final-composite tonemap are not shipped - six foreign passes restore `bindFramebuffer(null)` behind the renderer's back; the scene stays forward-tonemapped. `?air=off` the door. 7 pins; 38 mutants, 38 killed. EL4 THE FRAME SHIPPED (Mac, mid-arc: "Polished and exceptional detail. Proper darker dungeons. The goal isnt a half visioned system"): the EL3 departure was wrong on its facts - two restore sites in the tree, not six passes - so `render/renderTarget.js` grew the FRAME TARGET every pass restores to, the world draws into a canvas-sized frame image with its own depth, and the frame's first screen-space draw resolves it (the bright pass joining the emitters' bloom, a vignette, contrast about mid-grey, one encode); EYE ADAPTATION off the frame's mean log luminance (a 32x32 image's top mip, a 1x1 image stepped toward key/luminance: opening at 0.6/s into the dark, closing at 3/s into the light, clamped to [0.7, 1.8] so a dungeon stays dark once the eye has opened), read by every lane shader and the far ring; PROPER DARK DUNGEONS - the dungeon hosts' ambient and Better Ambience's trilight scaled to 0.35 under the lane, the interiors untouched; a Blinn-Phong glint from every lantern on the lit shaders. 6 pins; 35 mutants, 35 killed. **AUDIT-EL** (same day, Mac: "Audit"): two unanchored reviewer reads (GL and shaders; host wiring and light data flow) and two of my own (the frame lifecycle; the classic shaders' byte-identity) - TWENTY findings, all fixed and pinned (`test/audit_el.test.js`, 11; 33 mutants, 33 killed; the four tier campaigns re-run): the terrain gone under `?air=off` (uAO at unit 0 beside a sampler2DArray - INVALID_OPERATION at every draw), the eye's image unbound with the air off (an exposure read off the diffuse texture), the travel map's second beginFrame never resolved (WORLD_FRAME: a world frame spends the records, any other resolves the frame owed and keeps them), the composer's hardcoded sixteen that kept forty-eight from ever reaching the shader, the camera's depth image drawing every flat edge-on, the billboard shade's uniform nothing uploaded, the fog colour unscaled against the dark, panel frames through the lane's tonemap and eye, the biases in hyperbolic depth, the eye measuring its own output off one tap, the water surface's forty-eight, the sprite pass reading the world's AO, the flash as caster and glare, adaptation to a video's clear colour, and six small ones. The castle's darkening under the lane is a recorded judgement. NOT SEEN ON A GPU - Mac's eye is the gate. - `01-Overview/Audit-Roads.md` - SUPERSEDED before it was actioned (audit 2026-08-29, merge 2026-09-04: main had already replaced the road system whole with ROADS 22, Hazelnut's Basic Roads - the retired modules are marked on the page; the record stands). Was: COMPLETE, UNACTIONED (2026-08-29): THE ROAD DEEP AUDIT. 8 dimensions, 45 agents, 37 findings confirmed and 0 dropped at the verify stage (7 breaks / 19 degrades / 11 latent). THE LIVE DEFECT: a diagonal pixel seam SEVERS the road - the two painted bands touch at one corner point and share no edge, at 48% of all boundary crossings - which is what Mac saw as a road connected to nothing. It shipped green because the seam pin only ever drove N-S and E-W, the two orientations that cannot disagree, on blank tilemaps; and because the revert left stitchChain, the suite's only multi-pixel harness, as dead code. Confirmed sound and not to be re-audited: the router is optimal (0/546 suboptimal against an independent Dijkstra), generation is water-tight and connected over a 250-map fuzz, the bake is byte-deterministic four ways, and RG1 cannot tear a seam. Fix the diagonal seam first, writing its pin before the fix and driving it with a location on one side; then ROADS_V, one character, which silently withholds RC1 and RC3 from anyone who has already baked.

Was: PLANNED, not started (2026-08-29): THE THIRD ASSET-INJECTION DOMAIN. Mac's WindMills.rar study - model replacement (MeshReplacement.cs) and world-data overrides (WorldDataReplacement.cs), the two DFU injection domains the port lacks. The archive is the mod's Unity SOURCE project, not a built .dfmod, so the meshes are readable COLLADA rather than a sealed AssetBundle. Five findings settled: the watermill is 41601 not 21411 (LoadWindmill.cs is dead, absent from the manifest); the four "malformed" RMB JSONs are valid FullSerializer input (optional commas) and must NOT be repaired; the mesh axis rule is (-x, y, z) with reversed winding and the declared Z_UP is a lie (measured off the blade's mounting plank to 0.016); the mod's 21 PNGs must NOT be registered or they repaint 16 archives game-wide; and the one non-1:1 step is the two [ImportedComponent] behaviours, which are compiled C# and become named built-ins. 12 steps specified, 19 synthesized-fixture pins, the Unity prefab reader carrying the schedule risk (two shipped prefab shapes disagree structurally). First move (a) - does retail ARCH3D hold 41600/41601 - needs ARENA2_PATH and decides whether this ships as one feature or two.

Classic's title art is TITL00I0.IMG, which this port reads and pins byte-exact - but DFU does not draw it either: TITL00I0 appears nowhere in the DFU source except ImgFile's palettized file list, because DFU replaced classic's title with its OWN branding. Our analogue of that is our logo, so ui/titleScreen.js draws public/logo.png ahead of the menu - the FIRST non-ARENA2 image the port has ever loaded, and no breach of Port-Doctrine: game data still never enters the repo, but OUR artwork is ours and ships with the build. Centred, aspect-preserved against both axes, capped at 86% of the canvas over an OPAQUE black backdrop (the renderer clears to the pale Iliac Bay sky, which would otherwise frame the logo in blue), any click or key advances. NEVER TRAPS is the load-bearing law here: if the file is absent loadLogo resolves null, runTitle returns before it touches the renderer, and the boot goes straight to the menu - a missing asset costs a splash, never a game, the same rule the char sheet and chargen follow when their art fails. U21d then replaced 'nothing' with something real: the fallback is CLASSIC'S OWN TITLE. TITL00I0.IMG - the Daggerfall wordmark over the box art - comes out of the user's ARENA2 at runtime, so the port has a working title screen with nothing to ship and nothing to install, and swaps to ours the moment public/logo.png exists. The two arms draw by DIFFERENT laws on purpose: classic art takes the native panel at integer scale with the 1-bit cutout and NEAREST, ours takes logoRect with blending and LINEAR, and each law is wrong for the other's art. Probed live - classic's title at native scale on a black letterbox, with no logo file present. The artwork itself then forced two RENDERER opt-ins: our logo is not classic art, and the port's screen-quad law is a 1-BIT CUTOUT - discard a<0.5, force alpha 1 - which is exactly right for classic art, because a palettized IMG IS a 1-bit cutout with index 0 transparent and every other index opaque, and exactly wrong for anti-aliased gold serifs and the dagger's soft shadow, which it would jag and cut to a silhouette; likewise NEAREST/REPEAT keeps a 320x200 IMG pixel-exact at nativePanel's integer scales and aliases a 2000px banner drawn at a non-integer one. drawScreenQuad gained a { blend } arm and uploadTexture a { smooth } arm, BOTH caller opt-ins with titleScreen.js the only caller, so no game art path changed - pinned in both directions and mutation-proven four ways. Measured in the live shot: 65 distinct luminances across a feathered edge where the cutout would have given 2) SHIPPED. + THE MIDI.BSA SONG READER (2026-08-19: the ELEVENTH format reader, and the first written with NO DFU SOURCE AT ALL - DFU never reads this archive, it loads 133 pre-converted .mid files plus an SF2 from Unity Resources, so there is no reader to port and no reusable asset. Established from the shipped bytes of the user's own MIDI.BSA and corpus-gated over the whole retail archive. THE COMMISSION WAS WRONG AND THE BYTES SAID SO: this was briefed as an XMI reader, and there is no FORM/XDIR/CAT/ XMID chunk anywhere in the file - it is HMI Sound Operating System (signature HMI-MIDISONG061595), which shares exactly one trait with XMI, note-on carrying a duration, and differs in the delta encoding, ordinary MIDI VLQs rather than interval counts. Reading it as XMI desynchronises immediately. The file is named for the format it reads. 131 songs, 1286 tracks, all decoding; the load-bearing pin is the GLOBAL GATE - with the decoded event sizes every track consumes to its end-of-track landing BYTE-EXACTLY on the next track's offset, so a single wrong size drifts and fails. getSong returns a merged tick-ordered event stream a synth can walk. Sizes proven but meanings unknown are exposed RAW and say so, and anything unrecognised throws with song and offset rather than decoding to garbage. Verified independently before merge - the chunk-signature count, the song/track totals and the offset table at 0 mismatches, and the XMI-guess mutation killing 6 pins) SHIPPED. + U22 THE OPENING VIDEO (2026-08-19: VidFile.cs ported WHOLE - the tenth format reader and a TRUE 1:1, unlike the song reader that landed beside it, because this one has a DFU source and that one has none. ANIM0001.VID now plays ahead of the menu, where DaggerfallUI.InitGame puts it: the Start window is pushed and the VidPlayer pushed ON TOP, so the splash reveals the menu when it ends. Corpus-swept over all 17 retail VIDs with two exactness gates - decoded video blocks == header FrameCount, and final stream position == file size, from 98KB to 19.7MB, so a one-byte desync anywhere could not land on the last byte. FOUR DFU QUIRKS PRESERVED rather than smoothed (a mid-stream palette desyncs the stream, frameDelay is recomputed for every block so a video block inherits the last audio delay, a swallowed exception does not advance the stream so a bad block parks the reader forever, and Audio_StartFrame consumes its data before rejecting a bad rate) and ONE mechanism deliberately changed to keep behaviour: C#'s IndexOutOfRangeException on Color32[] is emulated with an explicit bounds throw, because a JS typed array drops an out-of-range write SILENTLY and a faithful transcription would have completed the very block C# aborts - the quirk would have been lost by porting the code correctly. Audio is ported, not deferred: the clip is built verbatim and scheduled on the WebAudio clock, though the boot splash plays SILENT until something resumes an AudioContext, since a browser will not start one before a gesture. The slice is TWO HALVES with two different standards of proof, and keeping them apart is the point. The PORTED half is SongManager.cs - the playlists verbatim (duplicates included, because a duplicate doubles that song's odds) and SelectCurrentSong arm by arm: taverns index gameDays directly so they walk in sequence day to day, dungeons seed DFRandom with unknown2 XOR (region<<8), everything else seeds on gameDays so a location's song is stable until midnight, and a list of ONE returns index 0 without consuming the generator - if it seeded, entering a temple would shift every later roll in the session. The OURS half is the synth, because DFU renders its own .mid files with a vendored synth and a SoundFont from Unity Resources: both DFU's assets, so there is nothing to port. 52 GM programs and channel-9 percussion means any sample answer ships megabytes or asks the player for an SF2, so the bank is oscillators - the only option that works the moment the page loads, and the worst sounding, so it sits behind an interface an SF2 replaces without touching the scheduler. Its specs are TASTE and are deliberately NOT pinned as truth: a pin saying 'program 48 is a sawtooth' would assert my own choices back at me, the vacuous-pin shape two audits have caught here, so the synth pins assert STRUCTURE - every GM program resolves, out-of-range clamps rather than throws, no percussion key is silent, and nothing can put a NaN in the audio graph. Music boots from the SAME seam as sound (shared.ensureAudio) for F6's reason - a second bootstrap is a second thing every host must remember - and the F6 pins were STRENGTHENED rather than relaxed to say so: one flag per subsystem, every subsystem behind the one seam. Verified by measurement, not by 'nothing threw' - tools/musicProbe.mjs taps an AnalyserNode in a real browser (peak RMS 0.056, all 40 frames audible) and tools/musicRender.mjs renders a WAV through the same production voice code so a human can listen. Mutation-proven eight ways. + A5b THE FM BANK AND ALL FOUR HOSTS, same day: the subtractive bank was replaced with TWO-OPERATOR FM, because that is the synthesis Daggerfall was scored for - AdLib/SoundBlaster OPL - and is why the archive carries an F*/FM* arrangement of nearly every song. Checked for a real OPL bank first and there is none: it lived in HMI's sound driver, ARENA2 has no .AD/.BNK/.OPL and no driver, and the song headers carry a device/channel map and zeros where patches would be. So the ratios stay ours on the same terms, but the METHOD is period-correct rather than arbitrary, and FM subsumes the old bank (index 0 is a bare carrier). Three behaviours are unreachable without that loop and are now live and pinned: a new DAY or a new LOCATION re-picks even when the playlist is the SAME OBJECT, locationIndex is in the context at all, and a finished song re-evaluates the context before the next is chosen. - **Mobile test build (2026-08-13, Mac-directed)**: deployed-site play
  on phones. `src/ui/touch.js` - a virtual stick synthesizing REAL
  W/A/S/D/Shift KeyboardEvents (the scenes' keys Sets and the input
  map see ordinary keys), right-half drag-look via a per-scene hook
  (touch can never hold pointer lock), an attack button mirroring the
  RMB-drag seam 1:1, and a button row (E/jump/F5/F6/spellbook/cast +
  a toggleable overlay-nav row with prompt()-based name entry).
  Data on phones: the picker overlay accepts a ZIP
  (DaggerfallGameFiles.zip or a zipped arena2) - a dependency-free
  reader (EOCD -> central dir -> DecompressionStream deflate-raw)
  with the arena2/ prefix filter; both real archives witnessed
  byte-exact. Viewport meta + touch-action CSS landed in index.html.
  Playwright touch probe (hasTouch context): UI activation, stick
  8-way + run throw, release clearing, look-drag clean. RESIDUAL
  (honest): the look hook's yaw effect is seam-verified only (walk
  mode exposes no yaw getter); the body is the literal mouse
  expression. Desktop untouched - the layer no-ops without touch.
